## gnews:8f5a03f1dde395491fc3814b · call 4
- luna-1 unmatched (12): e1: Anthropic introduced Claude Science, a scientific research workbench that connects to NVIDIA life-sciences models and workflows; the article gives no launch date in this sentence. ‖ e2: Anthropic announced the Claude Science beta on June 30, 2026, for Claude Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users. ‖ e3: Anthropic said Claude Science is available on macOS and Linux and can run locally, over SSH on remote machines, or through an HPC login node; no period was specified. ‖ e4: Claude Science’s coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors, Anthropic said; no period was specified. ‖ e5: Anthropic said Claude Science supports user-created specialist agents and includes a reviewer agent that checks citations and calculations and flags and corrects errors; no period was specified. ‖ e6: Anthropic said Claude Science figures include the generating code and environment, a process description, and message history, intended to make results easier to validate and reproduce; no period was specified. ‖ e7: NVIDIA announced its BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23, 2026. ‖ e8: Anthropic said Claude Science uses BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo life-sciences models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3; no period was specified. ‖ e9: NVIDIA described BioNeMo Agent Toolkit as domain-specific tools and skills for life-sciences agent workflows, including NVIDIA libraries, tools, and open models; no period was specified. ‖ e10: OpenAI announced plans to retire Custom GPTs and move associated specialized workflows to a plugin architecture spanning ChatGPT and Codex; no timing was specified. ‖ e11: Meta is returning to open-weight AI with Muse Glimmer, a model designed for consumer hardware; the article gives no date or size. ‖ e12: Microsoft unveiled Frontier Company and described it as a $2.5 billion bet on helping enterprises put AI models to work; no period was specified.

- luna-1 unmatched (10): e1: Valar Atomics announced a partnership with Nvidia to explore AI data centers powered directly by advanced nuclear energy; the article describes it as an industry first. ‖ e2: Valar Atomics and Nvidia are considering a 30-megawatt, nearly waterless data center in Utah using the Ward250 reactor and Nvidia cooling technology. ‖ e3: Valar Atomics said its reactor became the first startup-built reactor to generate nuclear power; the company was founded in 2023. ‖ e4: Valar Atomics' Ward250 reactor went critical on June 18, 2026, and was generating 100 kilowatts of electricity as of the article's publication. ‖ e5: President Donald Trump signed four executive orders in 2025 that the article says streamlined Valar Atomics' progress. ‖ e6: The proposed Valar Atomics-Nvidia data center is described as 300 times smaller than the approved Stratos data center in Box Elder County. ‖ e7: Nvidia recently announced its DSX AI factory design, which uses a water-efficient direct liquid cooling system. ‖ e8: Valar Atomics must obtain a Nuclear Regulatory Commission license before it can generate power commercially. ‖ e9: NRC chairman Ho Nieh said the commission hopes to shorten federal licensing review times to less than 18 months. ‖ e10: Valar Atomics CEO Isaiah Taylor said the Ward250's fuel would let the reactor continue running for 165 years.

- luna-1 unmatched (10): e3: The report said Firmus and Sharon AI had joined NVIDIA’s program, and three executives with NVIDIA business ties confirmed the arrangement. ‖ e4: NVIDIA announced on July 1, 2026, a business model combining revenue sharing and credit support for AI cloud providers purchasing NVIDIA infrastructure. ‖ e5: An NVIDIA spokesperson confirmed the program’s existence on July 1, 2026, as reported in the item. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said its model would generate a share of cloud-service revenue in addition to product revenue, creating a recurring, usage-based revenue stream. ‖ e7: NVIDIA described the framework as the DSX AI Factory model for cross-regional, high-utilization, multi-tenant AI computing services. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said emerging AI companies have faced capital constraints and that long-term contracts have often been insufficient to secure financing for compute capacity. ‖ e10: An unnamed data-center executive said NVIDIA’s purchase commitment for unsold compute capacity could address both GPU and data-center financing problems. ‖ e11: The report said Amazon, Microsoft, SpaceX, Oracle, Meta, and Google together purchased the majority of NVIDIA’s chip output. ‖ e13: The report said NVIDIA had invested several billion dollars in equity stakes in emerging cloud providers and entered chip leaseback transactions, including with CoreWeave and Lambda, totaling several billion dollars. ‖ e14: The Information had reported that NVIDIA researchers used GPU servers leased back from Lambda.
- luna-2 unmatched (9): e3: The report said Firmus and Sharon AI had joined the program, an arrangement confirmed by three executives with existing NVIDIA business ties. ‖ e4: NVIDIA announced on 2026-07-01 a business model combining revenue sharing and credit support to help AI cloud providers procure its infrastructure without paying all capital expenditure upfront. ‖ e5: An NVIDIA spokesperson confirmed the program existed. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said its model would add a share of cloud-service revenue to its product revenue, creating a usage-based recurring revenue stream. ‖ e7: NVIDIA’s model named Sharon AI and Firmus as its first participating cloud providers. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said emerging AI companies have faced financing constraints for capital-intensive infrastructure, and long-term contracts have often not secured financing for compute capacity. ‖ e10: The article said Amazon, Microsoft, SpaceX, Oracle, Meta, and Google together purchase the majority of NVIDIA’s chip output. ‖ e12: The article reported NVIDIA had invested several billion dollars in equity stakes in multiple emerging cloud providers and agreed in some cases to lease back chips, including from CoreWeave and Lambda, in transactions totaling several billion dollars. ‖ e13: The Information previously reported that NVIDIA researchers used GPU servers leased back from Lambda.
- luna-3 unmatched (10): e3: The report said Firmus and Sharon AI had joined NVIDIA’s program and that three executives with NVIDIA business ties confirmed the arrangement. ‖ e4: NVIDIA announced on 2026-07-01 a business model combining revenue sharing and credit support for AI cloud providers procuring its infrastructure. ‖ e5: The Information report said NVIDIA personnel called the initiative the “AI Compute Partnership,” and an NVIDIA spokesperson confirmed the program existed. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said its model would add a recurring, usage-based share of cloud service revenue to its product revenue. ‖ e7: NVIDIA described the framework as its “DSX AI Factory” model for continuous, cross-regional, multi-tenant accelerated computing. ‖ e8: NVIDIA identified Sharon AI and Firmus as the first providers participating in the model. ‖ e10: A data center executive characterized NVIDIA’s deals as “killing two birds with one stone.” ‖ e11: A data center executive said a commitment by NVIDIA to purchase unsold facility capacity would address both GPU and data-center financing problems. ‖ e13: Moomoo.com reported that NVIDIA had invested several billion dollars in equity stakes in emerging cloud providers and entered several billion dollars of transactions that included chip leasebacks from CoreWeave and Lambda. ‖ e14: The Information reported that NVIDIA researchers had used GPU servers leased back from Lambda.

## gnews:4f3a1d09e90b8fec9c86d2ba · call 17
- luna-1 unmatched (4): e1: TechRadar reported that RTX 5090 retail prices reached as much as twice the Founders Edition’s $2,000 launch MSRP. ‖ e2: TechRadar reported a $950 reference price for Intel’s Arc Pro B70 and retail/OEM prices of around $1,000 as of 2026-07-01. ‖ e3: TechRadar reported that a four-card Intel Arc Pro B70 configuration with 128GB total memory costs less than $3,800. ‖ e4: Puget Systems’ testing, as reported by TechRadar, found the RTX 5090 configuration 4–5 times faster in decode tasks and compared memory bandwidth of 1,792 GB/s versus 608 GB/s for the four-card Arc Pro B70 configuration.
- luna-2 unmatched (8): e1: TechRadar reported that Nvidia’s RTX 5090 has 21,760 CUDA cores and 32GB of GDDR7 memory. ‖ e2: TechRadar reported that RTX 5090 retail prices reached as much as 2 times the $2,000 Founders Edition launch MSRP in 2026, while naming the RTX 4090, 3090 Ti and 3090 as older alternatives with 24GB of VRAM. ‖ e3: TechRadar reported that Intel’s Arc Pro B70 has a $950 reference price, with most retailers and OEM partners selling SKUs for around $1,000. ‖ e4: TechRadar reported that the Intel Arc Pro B70 has 32GB of GDDR6 memory and costs about one quarter as much as most RTX 5090 SKUs on sale when the article was written. ‖ e5: TechRadar reported that Intel canceled the Arc B770 GPU, which had originally been slated to use Intel’s BMG-G31 “Big Battlemage” chip. ‖ e6: TechRadar reported that Puget Systems tested a four-card Arc Pro B70 setup against the RTX 5090, finding the RTX 5090 4–5 times faster in decode tasks and comparing memory bandwidth of 1,792 GB/s with 608 GB/s. ‖ e7: TechRadar reported Puget Systems’ assessment that compute- and bandwidth-intensive models favor the RTX 5090, while models requiring more memory for their parameters favor a B70 configuration on cost and available memory. ‖ e8: TechRadar reported that Intel’s oneAPI, OpenVINO and IPEX offerings are widely considered behind AMD’s ROCm, which it said lags Nvidia’s ecosystem.
- luna-3 unmatched (6): e1: TechRadar reported that the RTX 5090 has 21,760 CUDA cores and 32GB of GDDR7 memory. ‖ e2: TechRadar reported that RTX 5090 retail prices in 2026 reached as much as twice the $2,000 Founders Edition launch MSRP. ‖ e3: TechRadar reported an Intel Arc Pro B70 reference price of $950 and typical retailer and OEM prices of around $1,000 as of 2026-07-01. ‖ e4: TechRadar reported that the Intel Arc Pro B70 has 32GB of GDDR6 memory. ‖ e5: TechRadar reported that a four-card Intel Arc Pro B70 configuration with 128GB total memory costs less than $3,800. ‖ e6: TechRadar reported that Puget Systems' testing found the RTX 5090 4–5 times faster than four Arc Pro B70 cards in decode tasks, and compared memory bandwidth of 1,792 GB/s with 608 GB/s.
- haiku-1 unmatched (5): e1: Author reports RTX 5090 retail prices reaching as much as twice the $2,000 Founders Edition MSRP at launch, in 2026. ‖ e2: Intel's Arc Pro B70 carries a reference price of $950. ‖ e3: Author reports most retailers and OEM partners sell Arc Pro B70 SKUs at around $1,000. ‖ e4: Puget Systems testing finds the RTX 5090 4-5x faster than four Arc Pro B70 cards in decode tasks, per the author's account. ‖ e5: Author states a 128GB four-card Arc Pro B70 configuration costs less than $3,800.
- haiku-2 unmatched (4): e1: TechRadar reports RTX 5090 retail prices reached as much as twice the $2,000 Founders Edition MSRP at launch. ‖ e2: TechRadar says the Arc Pro B70 at about $1,000 is roughly a quarter of the price of most RTX 5090 SKUs on sale at time of writing. ‖ e3: Intel Arc Pro B70 has a $950 reference price, with most retailers and OEM partners selling it at around $1,000. ‖ e4: Puget Systems testing finds the RTX 5090 4-5x faster than Arc Pro B70 configurations in decode tasks, per TechRadar's report.

## gnews:87742e41d1edc4e292c234fe · call 20
- luna-1 unmatched (10): e1: Palantir CEO Alex Karp said on Wednesday that OpenAI and Anthropic reportedly lack measures to protect clients' IP. ‖ e2: Alex Karp said Palantir believes warfighters and U.S. allies should have access to the best technology available. ‖ e3: Alex Karp said private-sector enterprises want control over access to their data and model weights, and connected those concerns to the Palantir–Nvidia partnership. ‖ e4: Alex Karp said Palantir’s technical customers want control over their compute, models, data stack, and “alpha,” and that this underpins Palantir’s alignment with Nvidia. ‖ e5: Alex Karp said trust needs to be rebuilt and said Palantir sells a product that lets customers switch between models. ‖ e6: Palantir announced earlier in the week that it was integrating Nvidia’s Nemotron open AI models into its intelligent engine for mission-specific sovereign AI for U.S. government agencies and critical infrastructure operators. ‖ e7: Palantir said its system enables deployment of frontier-level AI in secure, air-gapped environments while keeping sensitive data off public networks. ‖ e8: Palantir’s new platform allows agencies to customize Nemotron models with their data, retain ownership of resulting weights, and improve performance through feedback in their infrastructure. ‖ e9: Palantir said its Sovereign AI Operating System, built on AIP, Foundry, Ontology, and Apollo, provides security, authorization, and audit capabilities for sensitive government deployments. ‖ e10: Nvidia and Palantir said combining Nvidia’s open models with Palantir’s software gives agencies and enterprises greater transparency, customization, and cost efficiency than closed AI models.
- luna-2 unmatched (2): e1: Palantir announced earlier in the week of 2026-07-01 that it was integrating Nvidia Nemotron models into its intelligent engine for sovereign AI offerings to U.S. government agencies and critical infrastructure operators. ‖ e2: Palantir CEO Alex Karp said in a CNBC interview that OpenAI and Anthropic reportedly lack measures to protect clients’ intellectual property; the article gives no exact interview day.
- luna-3 unmatched (5): e1: Palantir CEO Alex Karp argued in a CNBC interview on Wednesday, 2026-07-01, that OpenAI and Anthropic reportedly lack measures to protect clients' IP. ‖ e2: Palantir announced earlier in the week of 2026-07-01 that it is integrating Nvidia Nemotron open AI models into its intelligent engine for U.S. government agencies and critical-infrastructure operators. ‖ e3: Palantir said its system enables organizations to deploy frontier-level AI in secure, air-gapped environments while keeping sensitive data off public networks; no deployment quantity or period was specified. ‖ e4: Palantir said its new platform lets agencies customize Nemotron models with their data, retain ownership of resulting model weights, and improve performance through feedback on their infrastructure. ‖ e5: Palantir and Nvidia said combining Nvidia open models with Palantir software gives agencies and enterprises greater transparency, customization, and cost efficiency than closed AI models.
- haiku-1 unmatched (1): e1: Palantir announced it is integrating Nvidia's Nemotron open AI models into its new intelligent engine for U.S. government agencies and critical infrastructure operators, earlier in the week of 2026-07-01; no contract value or size given.
- haiku-2 unmatched (1): e1: Palantir announced it is integrating Nvidia's Nemotron open AI models into its new intelligent engine for U.S. government agencies and critical infrastructure operators; no size or value given.

- luna-1 unmatched (8): e1: Nvidia announced a revenue-sharing program on Wednesday, July 1, 2026, to provide AI startups, model builders, and enterprises access to its computing infrastructure without requiring them to finance hardware buildouts. ‖ e3: Nvidia CFO Colette Kress said in a blog post on July 1, 2026, that the arrangement would provide Nvidia with a recurring, usage-linked earnings stream and let customers access large-scale compute without first handling site selection, power procurement, construction, and hardware bring-up. ‖ e4: Nvidia's offering includes token credits that let startups access compute resources without upfront capital outlays. ‖ e7: Nvidia said Baseten, Fireworks AI, and Together AI are examples of the AI-native firms its program is designed to serve. ‖ e8: Nvidia said on July 2, 2026, that its program responds to a shift in AI demand from model development toward production inference workloads requiring continuously operating infrastructure at scale. ‖ e9: Nvidia said emerging AI companies have historically had limited access to capital-intensive compute resources and that even long-term commitments have not been sufficient to unlock financing. ‖ e10: Alphabet and Amazon have been offering customers access to their own processors, according to the article. ‖ e11: Nvidia has taken equity stakes in neocloud operators including CoreWeave and Nebius.
- luna-2 unmatched (4): e1: Nvidia announced a revenue-sharing program on Wednesday, 2026-07-01, to provide AI startups, model builders, and enterprises access to its computing infrastructure without requiring them to finance hardware buildouts. ‖ e3: Kress said in a blog post that the program would create a “recurring, usage-linked earnings stream” for Nvidia and remove several infrastructure steps for customers; she gave no amount or period. ‖ e6: Nvidia characterized AI demand as shifting from model development toward production inference workloads requiring continuously operating infrastructure at scale; it gave no quantified estimate. ‖ e7: Nvidia said emerging AI companies have historically had limited access to capital-intensive compute and that long-term commitments could be insufficient to secure financing; no amount or period was specified.
- luna-3 unmatched (9): e1: Nvidia announced a revenue-sharing program on Wednesday, July 1, 2026, intended to give AI startups, model builders, and enterprises access to its computing infrastructure without financing hardware buildouts. ‖ e3: Nvidia CFO Colette Kress described the program's income for Nvidia as a “recurring, usage-linked earnings stream” and said it lets customers access large-scale compute without first handling site selection, power procurement, construction, and hardware bring-up. ‖ e4: The program also offers token credits intended to let startups access compute without upfront capital outlays. ‖ e5: Nvidia named Sharon AI and Firmus Technologies as the program's two initial partners. ‖ e8: Nvidia named Baseten, Fireworks AI, and Together AI as examples of the AI-native firms the program is designed to serve. ‖ e9: Sharon AI CEO James Manning described the collaboration with Nvidia as a pivotal moment in the company's mission to deliver sovereign, large-scale AI compute infrastructure. ‖ e10: Firmus Technologies co-CEO Tim Rosenfield said AI-native companies need scalable, energy- and cost-efficient compute infrastructure to compete globally. ‖ e11: Nvidia characterized AI demand as shifting from model development toward production inference workloads requiring continuously operating infrastructure at scale. ‖ e12: Nvidia said emerging AI companies have historically had limited access to capital-intensive compute, and that long-term commitments have not necessarily been enough to obtain financing.

- luna-2 unmatched (6): e1: Nvidia announced a model to earn hardware revenue and an ongoing percentage of partner AI-cloud revenue generated using that hardware; no term or percentage is specified here. ‖ e2: Nvidia named Australia's Sharon AI and Singapore-based Firmus Technologies as the first partners in its revenue-sharing and credit-support model. ‖ e4: Bloomberg reported that developers receive token credits for a share of future sales; the revenue-split percentages had not been disclosed as of 2026-07-02. ‖ e6: Sharon AI disclosed in its CY25 results a revenue-share facility of up to $200 million with Digital Alpha. ‖ e7: Firmus is building a DSX-aligned campus in Batam expected to scale to 360 MW and house up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ e9: The article characterized Nvidia's gross margins as roughly 75%; it did not specify the period or accounting basis.
- luna-3 unmatched (8): e1: Nvidia announced a model in which it receives hardware revenue and an ongoing share of partner AI-cloud revenue generated using that hardware; no period or percentage was specified. ‖ e2: Nvidia named Australia's Sharon AI and Singapore's Firmus Technologies as the first partners in the model. ‖ e3: Bloomberg reported that developers receive token credits for a share of future sales; Nvidia and its partners had not disclosed the split percentages as of 2026-07-02. ‖ e4: Sharon AI disclosed in a June 12, 2026 8-K that its agreement runs for six years. ‖ e5: Sharon AI's agreement covers 72 MW of new Australian data-center capacity built to Nvidia's DSX AI factory design. ‖ e7: Sharon AI disclosed in its CY25 results a revenue-share facility of up to $200 million with investor Digital Alpha. ‖ e8: Firmus is building a DSX-aligned campus in Batam, Indonesia, expected to scale to 360 MW. ‖ e9: The Firmus campus is expected to house up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs.
- haiku-1 unmatched (6): e1: Nvidia announced a revenue-sharing and credit-support model in a blog post co-authored by CFO Colette Kress, pitched as a way to give startups that cannot self-finance access to compute. ‖ e2: Sharon AI and Firmus are the first partners in the model, with up to 210,000 GPUs combined. ‖ e4: Sharon AI holds a revenue-share facility of up to $200 million with investor Digital Alpha, disclosed in its CY25 results. ‖ e5: Firmus is building a DSX-aligned campus in Batam, Indonesia, expected to scale to 360 MW and house up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ e6: Per Bloomberg, developers receive token credits in exchange for a slice of future sales; the revenue-split percentages have not been disclosed. ‖ e8: Nvidia is described as already running roughly 75% gross margins.
- haiku-2 unmatched (5): e1: Nvidia announced a revenue-sharing and credit-support model for AI clouds in a blog post co-authored by CFO Colette Kress, dated 2026-07-02. ‖ e3: Per Bloomberg, developers receive token credits for a share of future sales, but the revenue-split percentages have not been disclosed by Nvidia or its partners. ‖ e5: Sharon AI holds a revenue-share facility of up to $200 million with investor Digital Alpha, disclosed in its CY25 results. ‖ e6: Firmus is building a DSX-aligned campus in Batam, Indonesia, expected to scale to 360 MW and house up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ e9: The journalist states Nvidia is already running roughly 75% gross margins.
- haiku-3 unmatched (7): ev1: Nvidia's blog post, co-authored by CFO Colette Kress, pitched a revenue-sharing and credit-support model to open compute access to startups. ‖ ev2: Sharon AI and Firmus are the first partners in the model, with a combined total of up to 210,000 GPUs. ‖ ev4: Per Bloomberg, developers receive token credits in exchange for a slice of their future sales. ‖ ev5: Neither Nvidia nor its partners has disclosed the revenue-split percentages under the model. ‖ ev8: Sharon AI holds a revenue-share facility of up to $200 million with investor Digital Alpha, disclosed in its CY25 results. ‖ ev9: Firmus's DSX-aligned Batam, Indonesia campus is expected to scale to 360 MW and house up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ ev12: The reporter describes Nvidia as already running roughly 75% gross margins.

- luna-2 candidates: binding_obligation/financing_amount: At Nvidia's FY2027 annual filing, what amount of financing or credit-support obligations has Nvidia taken on under its AI-cloud revenue-sharing model? (due 2027-02-28)

- luna-1 unmatched (16): e1: Nvidia told the U.S. government in a report presented last year that Huawei’s chip stockpile and SMIC’s growing capacity could give Huawei the flexibility to satisfy global AI chip demand. ‖ e2: Nvidia’s report to the U.S. government said Huawei production and sales would soar if Nvidia were kept out of China, without specifying a timeframe. ‖ e3: President Trump announced in December 2025 that his administration would allow Nvidia to sell H200 chips to China. ‖ e4: The Biden administration’s export controls previously restricted Nvidia from selling H200 chips to China; Nvidia had launched the chip in 2023. ‖ e5: Jensen Huang said Nvidia’s China market share had fallen to zero since the Biden administration first imposed AI-chip export controls in 2022. ‖ e8: Nvidia’s report estimated that SMIC would be producing several million Huawei 910B chips by 2027. ‖ e9: Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick estimated in congressional testimony in June 2025 that China could make 200,000 advanced chips in calendar 2025. ‖ e10: An Nvidia spokesperson said China’s domestic ecosystem can yield and scale competitive AI systems, pointing to leading Chinese models optimized for domestic hardware. ‖ e11: Chris McGuire estimated in a December report that Huawei making 800,000 Ascend 910C chips in 2025 would equal 5.3% of Nvidia’s total chip-production processing power that year. ‖ e12: Chris McGuire estimated that Nvidia’s performance lead would grow even if Huawei made 2 million AI chips in 2026 and 4 million in 2027. ‖ e13: Nvidia’s report cited Reuters reporting that Huawei had stockpiled 2–3 million AI chips made by TSMC and obtained through alleged illegal diversion by Sophgo. ‖ e14: U.S. export controls effectively prevent companies such as TSMC from selling to Huawei chips made using American technology. ‖ e15: Lennart Heim estimated that U.S. production would exceed 60 million 910C-equivalent chips from January 2025 through December 2026. ‖ e17: Huawei introduced its 950PR chip in March 2026 and was preparing to launch another chip later in 2026. ‖ e19: An Nvidia spokesperson said the company sells its first, best and most technology to the U.S. and offers competitive products in China to commercial users vetted and approved by the U.S. government. ‖ e20: An Nvidia spokesperson said national security takes precedence over commercial opportunities that conflict with it.

- haiku-1 unmatched (5): e2: Valar Atomics announced at a live event its partnership with Nvidia to power an AI factory with its Ward 250 microreactor. ‖ e3: During the on-stage demonstration, the Ward 250 reactor was run at 37% of full power to power a Blackwell-based Nvidia RTX desktop PC. ‖ e4: CEO Isiah Taylor said the reactor produces 100 kilowatts of thermal energy. ‖ e5: The Department of Energy says Deployable Energy's Unity and Antares Nuclear's Mark-0 have also achieved criticality, so Valar's claim to be first to produce power is disputed. ‖ e7: The article says Amazon, Microsoft and Nvidia are working on technologies that could cut data center water use by up to 100%.
- haiku-2 unmatched (6): e1: Valar Atomics said it activated its Ward 250 microreactor live on stage and announced a partnership with Nvidia to power an AI factory. ‖ e2: Valar Atomics' reactor was turned up to 37% of full power to run a Blackwell-powered Nvidia RTX desktop PC during the live demonstration. ‖ e3: CEO Isiah Taylor said the reactor produces 100 kilowatts of thermal energy from uranium fission. ‖ e4: The Department of Energy says two other firms, Deployable Energy's Unity and Antares Nuclear's Mark-0, have achieved criticality. ‖ e5: A survey reported that 7 in 10 Americans do not want a data center in their backyard. ‖ e7: Amazon, Microsoft and Nvidia are reported to be working on technologies that could cut data center water use by up to 100%.
- haiku-3 unmatched (7): e1: Valar Atomics activated its Ward 250 microreactor live on stage and announced a partnership with Nvidia to power an AI factory. ‖ e2: The Ward 250 reactor was raised to 37% of its full power to run a Blackwell-powered Nvidia RTX desktop PC on stage. ‖ e3: CEO Isiah Taylor said the reactor produces 100 kilowatts of thermal energy. ‖ e4: CEO Isiah Taylor claims anyone can access the website, which is run solely from a server powered by the reactor, while the reactor is running. ‖ e5: The Department of Energy says Deployable Energy's Unity and Antares Nuclear's Mark-0 have also achieved criticality. ‖ e7: A survey found 7 in 10 Americans do not want a data center in their backyard. ‖ e9: Amazon, Microsoft and Nvidia are reportedly working on technologies that could cut data center water use by up to 100%.

- luna-1 unmatched (13): e1: Greenpeace East Asia and Alistair Alexander claim that Taiwan and South Korea bear over 90% of the environmental burden from the semiconductor industry. ‖ e3: The authors say Nvidia's supply-chain emissions more than doubled over the past three years and forecast GPU shipments to grow 26% per year. ‖ e4: The authors say Taiwan hosts more than 90% of global leading-edge chip manufacturing. ‖ e6: The authors say TSMC operates more than two dozen fabs in Taiwan and plans 10 additional advanced fabs like Fab 25. ‖ e8: The authors report that Taipower plans 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, around 40% of Taiwan's current generating capacity, largely to serve semiconductor-industry demand. ‖ e9: The authors say fossil fuels provide more than 80% of Taiwan's electricity. ‖ e10: The authors say Samsung and SK Hynix are pursuing expansions and are key partners in South Korea's Yongin Mega-cluster plan for semiconductor fabs and AI factories. ‖ e11: The authors say the Yongin plan calls for six LNG units totaling 3 GW by 2030. ‖ e12: The authors estimate Yongin will ultimately demand about 15 GW of electricity, roughly one-sixth of South Korea's total demand, and use about half as much water as Seoul. ‖ e13: South Korean President Lee Jae-myung said the country must move very quickly toward renewable energy. ‖ e14: Greenpeace's energy team warned that South Korea may revert to coal rather than accelerate renewables as industrial energy demand rises. ‖ e15: Ahead of Nvidia's June 24 annual meeting, shareholders proposed that the company report on its products' lifecycle carbon emissions after sale. ‖ e16: Nvidia recommended that shareholders vote against the lifecycle-emissions reporting proposal, calling it “too prescriptive.”
- luna-2 unmatched (18): e1: Greenpeace East Asia and Alistair Alexander said Taiwan and South Korea bear over 90 percent of the environmental burden from making successive generations of microchips. ‖ e2: Greenpeace activists in Taiwan intercepted Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang during his late-May visit and delivered him a five-layer cake bearing the message “AI Needs Renewable Energy.” ‖ e3: Jensen Huang said in recent industry remarks that energy is the foundational layer of AI development. ‖ e4: The authors said Taiwan hosts more than 90 percent of global leading-edge chip manufacturing. ‖ e6: The authors said Nvidia's supply-chain emissions more than doubled over the three years before publication. ‖ e7: The authors said Nvidia GPU shipments were forecast to grow 26 percent per year; the forecast period was not specified. ‖ e9: The authors said TSMC operated more than two dozen fabs in Taiwan and was planning 10 more advanced fabs like Fab 25. ‖ e11: The authors said Taiwan utility Taipower planned 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, largely to meet semiconductor-industry demand. ‖ e12: The authors said fossil fuels supplied more than 80 percent of Taiwan's electricity. ‖ e13: The authors said Samsung and SK Hynix were pursuing expansions to meet chip demand and produce most of Nvidia's memory chips. ‖ e14: The authors said Samsung and SK Hynix were key partners in South Korea's Yongin Mega-cluster plan for semiconductor fabs and AI factories. ‖ e15: The authors said the Yongin Mega-cluster plan called for six LNG power units totaling 3 GW by 2030. ‖ e16: The authors said the Yongin complex would ultimately require about 15 GW of electricity and about half as much water as Seoul uses. ‖ e17: The authors reported that Taiwan said it had secured energy supplies through the end of 2026. ‖ e18: South Korean President Lee Jae-myung said the country must move very quickly toward renewable energy. ‖ e19: Greenpeace's energy team warned that South Korea might revert to coal rather than accelerate renewables amid rising industrial demand. ‖ e20: Ahead of Nvidia's June 24, 2026 annual meeting, shareholders proposed that the company report products' lifecycle carbon emissions after sale. ‖ e21: Nvidia recommended that shareholders vote against the lifecycle-emissions reporting proposal, calling it “too prescriptive.”
- luna-3 unmatched (12): e1: Greenpeace activists intercepted Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang during his Taiwan visit in late May 2026 and delivered him a five-layer model cake. ‖ e3: The article says Nvidia’s supply-chain emissions more than doubled over the three years preceding publication and forecasts GPU shipments growing 26% per year; the forecast period is unspecified. ‖ e5: The article says TSMC operates more than two dozen fabs in Taiwan and is planning 10 additional advanced fabs; the schedule is unspecified. ‖ e7: Taipower plans 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, which the article says is roughly 40% of Taiwan’s current generating capacity; the buildout schedule is unspecified. ‖ e8: The article says fossil fuels supply more than 80% of Taiwan’s electricity; the period is unspecified. ‖ e9: The article identifies Samsung and SK Hynix as key partners in South Korea’s Yongin Mega-cluster plan for semiconductor fabs and AI factories; the plan’s schedule is unspecified. ‖ e10: The article says the Yongin plan calls for six LNG units totaling 3 GW by 2030. ‖ e11: The article estimates Yongin will ultimately demand about 15 GW of electricity and use about half as much water as Seoul; the timing is unspecified. ‖ e12: South Korean President Lee Jae-myung said the country must move very quickly toward renewable energy; no date or quantified target is given. ‖ e13: Greenpeace’s energy team warned that South Korea may return to coal instead of accelerating renewables, citing rising industrial demand; no amount or deadline is specified. ‖ e14: Ahead of Nvidia’s June 24, 2026 annual meeting, shareholders proposed that the company report its products’ post-sale lifecycle carbon emissions. ‖ e15: Nvidia recommended that shareholders vote against the lifecycle-emissions reporting proposal, describing it as “too prescriptive”; the recommendation was made ahead of the June 24, 2026 annual meeting.
- haiku-1 unmatched (7): e2: Article authors state that Nvidia's supply chain emissions more than doubled over the past three years; no source or figure given. ‖ e3: Unattributed forecast that Nvidia's GPU shipments will grow 26% per year. ‖ e6: Taipower plans 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, largely to meet semiconductor demand led by TSMC, per the article. ‖ e7: Yongin Mega-cluster plan calls for six LNG units (3 GW) of power by 2030. ‖ e8: Yongin complex is expected to ultimately demand about 15 GW of electricity, roughly one-sixth of South Korea's demand. ‖ e9: Nvidia recommended shareholders vote against a proposal to report lifecycle carbon emissions of its products after sale, calling it too prescriptive, ahead of its June 24 annual meeting. ‖ e10: Article states Taiwan hosts more than 90% of global leading-edge chip manufacturing, a critical base for Nvidia.
- haiku-2 unmatched (6): e2: The authors state Nvidia's supply chain emissions more than doubled over the past three years. ‖ e3: Nvidia GPU shipments are forecast to grow 26% per year, with no source named. ‖ e6: Taipower plans 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, largely to meet semiconductor demand led by TSMC. ‖ e7: South Korea's Yongin Mega-cluster plan calls for 3 GW from six LNG units by 2030 and will ultimately demand about 15 GW. ‖ e8: Nvidia recommended shareholders vote against a proposal to report lifecycle carbon emissions of products after sale, calling it too prescriptive, ahead of its June 24 annual meeting. ‖ e9: The authors describe Taiwan as hosting more than 90% of global leading-edge chip manufacturing.
- haiku-3 unmatched (9): e2: Greenpeace East Asia and the article author state that Nvidia's supply chain emissions more than doubled over the past three years. ‖ e3: An unnamed forecast says Nvidia's GPU shipments will grow 26 percent per year. ‖ e5: TSMC's planned Fab 25 will use about 100,000 tonnes of water per day, roughly a fifth of Taichung's daily water use. ‖ e7: Taipower plans to build 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, largely to meet semiconductor demand led by TSMC. ‖ e8: South Korea's Yongin Mega-cluster plan calls for 3 GW from six LNG units by 2030. ‖ e9: The Yongin Mega-cluster will ultimately demand about 15 GW of electricity, roughly one-sixth of South Korea's total demand. ‖ e10: Nvidia recommended shareholders vote against a proposal to report lifecycle carbon emissions of its products, ahead of its June 24 annual meeting. ‖ e11: Taiwan says it has secured energy supplies through the end of 2026. ‖ e12: The article states Taiwan hosts more than 90 percent of global leading-edge chip manufacturing.

- luna-1 unmatched (17): e2: The Singapore Police Force said it issued a prohibition of disposal order against a residence valued at around S$55 million ($42.4 million) and seized around S$1 million in funds in connection with fraud investigations. ‖ e3: Aaron Woon and Alan Wei were charged with criminal conspiracy to commit fraud, and Li Ming was charged with fraud by false representation, in February 2025. ‖ e4: Jenny Lim was charged in connection with the case in April 2026. ‖ e5: Investigators allege that Aperia International, A-Speed Infotech, and Aperia Cloud Services concealed the actual end-user of servers; Woon, Wei, and Lim were key officers of the companies at the time of the alleged offences. ‖ e6: Investigators found Li to be the controller of Luxuriate Your Life, which was allegedly involved in the scheme. ‖ e8: Police announced additional fraud and money-laundering charges on July 1, 2026: seven each for Lim and Woon, and seven further charges for Wei scheduled for July 6. ‖ e9: Fraud charges allege Woon, Wei, and Lim conspired to commit fraud in purchasing servers from Dell, Super Micro Computer, and Asus. ‖ e10: The charges allege that Woon, Wei, and Lim falsely represented to suppliers from November 2023 through February 2025 that an Aperia Group company would be the servers' end-user. ‖ e11: Four companies are due to face eight fraud-by-false-representation charges on July 6, 2026; the SPF described this as the first prosecution of corporate entities in these investigations. ‖ e12: Police said Lim and Woon were charged with money laundering after each acquired approximately S$1.2 million in personal bank accounts, which police said pointed to benefits from criminal conduct. ‖ e13: Wei faces three money-laundering counts relating to the seized mansion and an additional S$5.8 deposited in his personal account; police allege the deposit was partly proceeds of criminal conduct. ‖ e14: Li faced additional fraud charges alleging misrepresentation of the end-user of servers Luxuriate Your Life intended to buy from Super Micro. ‖ e15: In February 2025, media reported that the U.S. Commerce Department was investigating whether DeepSeek had obtained restricted Nvidia chips through third parties in countries including Malaysia and Singapore. ‖ e16: Singapore said it was unaware of smuggling in the country and would act firmly against individuals or entities found to breach the law. ‖ e17: The Singapore Police Force said it takes a zero-tolerance stance toward such offenses and will act against businesses or individuals who violate Singaporean law. ‖ e18: Malaysian authorities said they foiled an attempt to smuggle advanced AI chips through the country's main airport the previous week, seizing 72 servers worth nearly $13 million. ‖ e19: Singapore police reported that the number of such cases rose from 589 in the first half of 2024 to 1,762 in the first half of 2026.

- luna-1 unmatched (9): e1: NVIDIA updated its licensing documentation to make Omniverse free for development and production use as of May 2026, and posted an announcement on July 1, 2026. ‖ e2: NVIDIA AI Enterprise, which previously was required for Omniverse production use, was listed at $4,500 per GPU per year, $22,500 per GPU perpetual, or $1 per GPU-hour on CSP marketplaces. ‖ e3: NVIDIA’s new Omniverse terms remove the AI Enterprise subscription requirement for production use and allow Omniverse-built products to be redistributed under the free terms. ‖ e5: NVIDIA’s embedded licensing model allows commercial partners redistributing Omniverse to provide front-line support, with NVIDIA providing back-line support. ‖ e6: NVIDIA introduced its Alpamayo family of open autonomous vehicle models at CES 2026. ‖ e7: NVIDIA’s Alpamayo 1 is a 10-billion-parameter vision-language-action model built on Cosmos. ‖ e8: The Mercedes-Benz CLA on NVIDIA’s DRIVE platform reached the US market in 2026 as the first production deployment described in the article. ‖ e9: NVIDIA released the Omniverse DSX Blueprint alongside the Vera Rubin DSX AI factory reference design in March 2026; the framework is for digital twins of gigawatt-scale GPU data centers. ‖ e10: Cadence, Schneider Electric, Siemens, Vertiv, Eaton, Jacobs, and other partners contributed SimReady assets and integrated design tools for NVIDIA’s Omniverse DSX Blueprint.
- luna-2 unmatched (12): e1: NVIDIA updated its licensing documentation to make Omniverse free for development and production as of May 2026, and posted an announcement on July 1, 2026. ‖ e2: NVIDIA had OpenUSD, neural rendering, and physical AI sessions scheduled for SIGGRAPH 2026, running July 19–23 in Los Angeles. ‖ e3: Under the prior model, production Omniverse deployments required NVIDIA AI Enterprise, listed at $4,500 per GPU per year, $22,500 per GPU for a perpetual license, or $1 per GPU-hour on CSP marketplaces. ‖ e4: NVIDIA's new terms remove the AI Enterprise subscription requirement for production use and allow Omniverse-built products to be redistributed under the free terms. ‖ e6: NVIDIA's embedded licensing model lets partners redistribute Omniverse in commercial products, with partners providing front-line support and NVIDIA providing back-line support. ‖ e7: NVIDIA introduced its Alpamayo family of open autonomous vehicle models at CES 2026. ‖ e8: NVIDIA's Alpamayo 1 is a 10-billion-parameter vision-language-action model built on Cosmos. ‖ e9: The Mercedes-Benz CLA, the first production deployment on NVIDIA's DRIVE platform, reached the U.S. market in 2026. ‖ e10: NVIDIA stated an ambition for every factory to have a digital twin before ground is broken. ‖ e11: NVIDIA released the Omniverse DSX Blueprint alongside the Vera Rubin DSX AI factory reference design in March 2026; the framework is for digital twins of gigawatt-scale GPU data centers. ‖ e12: Cadence, Schneider Electric, Siemens, Vertiv, Eaton, Jacobs, and other partners contributed SimReady assets and integrated design tools for NVIDIA's Omniverse DSX Blueprint. ‖ e13: StorageReview characterized AI factory buildouts as costing billions of dollars.

- luna-1 unmatched (9): e1: Nvidia announced the AI Compute Partnership on July 1, 2026, to backstop neocloud GPU deployments in exchange for a recurring share of cloud revenue. ‖ e3: Nvidia did not disclose the revenue-share percentage or provide details beyond its blog post. ‖ e4: Nvidia described the partnership as providing “economic alignment with a revenue-sharing and credit-support model.” ‖ e5: Sharon AI planned to deploy up to 40,000 Grace Blackwell GB300 GPUs. ‖ e6: Sharon AI listed on Nasdaq in February 2026 through a $125 million IPO. ‖ e8: Sharon AI partnered with VAST Data for 600PB of storage infrastructure supporting roughly 100,000 GPUs across its Australia and Asia-Pacific sovereign cloud operations. ‖ e9: Firmus Technologies was building a 360MW campus in Batam, Indonesia, targeting up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ e10: Sharon AI and Firmus Technologies planned to serve Baseten, Fireworks AI, Together AI, and enterprise inference workloads. ‖ e11: CoreWeave secured $6.3 billion and Lambda secured $1.5 billion through debt-financing arrangements in which Nvidia played a supporting role.
- luna-2 unmatched (8): e1: Nvidia unveiled the AI Compute Partnership on July 1, 2026, to backstop neocloud GPU deployments in exchange for a recurring share of revenue from the supported capacity. ‖ e2: The AI Compute Partnership's first two adopters are Sharon AI, planning up to 40,000 Grace Blackwell GB300 GPUs, and Firmus Technologies, building a 360MW Batam campus targeting up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ e3: Nvidia described the partnership as a revenue-sharing and credit-support model in a blog post co-authored by CFO Colette Kress. ‖ e4: Nvidia did not disclose the revenue-share percentage or provide further details beyond its blog post. ‖ e6: Nvidia previously provided demand guarantees to help neoclouds including CoreWeave and Lambda raise debt financing. ‖ e11: Sharon AI and Firmus Technologies plan to serve Baseten, Fireworks AI, Together AI, and enterprise inference workloads. ‖ e12: CoreWeave secured $6.3 billion and Lambda secured $1.5 billion through arrangements in which Nvidia played a supporting role. ‖ e13: Nvidia's blog post characterized the arrangement as providing a recurring, usage-linked earnings stream beyond one-time chip sales.
- luna-3 unmatched (6): e1: Nvidia unveiled the AI Compute Partnership on July 1, 2026, describing a model that backstops neocloud GPU deployments in return for a recurring share of cloud revenue. ‖ e2: Sharon AI planned to deploy up to 40,000 Grace Blackwell GB300 GPUs, while Firmus Technologies was building a 360MW Batam campus targeting up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs. ‖ e3: Nvidia described the AI Compute Partnership as providing economic alignment through revenue sharing and credit support. ‖ e4: Nvidia did not disclose the revenue-share percentage or provide further details beyond its blog post. ‖ e7: Nvidia said the program is intended to accelerate adoption of its platforms among AI-native companies. ‖ e11: CoreWeave secured $6.3 billion and Lambda $1.5 billion through debt arrangements in which Nvidia reportedly provided demand guarantees and customer referrals.
- haiku-1 unmatched (5): e2: Sharon AI plans to deploy up to 40,000 GB300 GPUs in Australia under a six-year agreement, the first adopter of the Nvidia program. ‖ e3: Firmus Technologies targets up to 170,000 Nvidia GPUs in a 360MW facility in Batam, Indonesia, a second adopter of the Nvidia program. ‖ e4: Nvidia did not disclose the revenue-share percentage and declined to give further details beyond its blog post co-authored by CFO Colette Kress. ‖ e6: Media report that CoreWeave secured $6.3 billion and Lambda $1.5 billion of debt through arrangements in which Nvidia provided demand guarantees or referrals. ‖ e7: Sharon AI has partnered with VAST Data for 600PB of storage supporting roughly 100,000 GPUs across its sovereign cloud operations.
- haiku-2 unmatched (5): e2: Sharon AI plans to deploy up to 40,000 GB300 GPUs in Australia under a six-year agreement, the first AI Compute Partnership adopter. ‖ e3: Firmus Technologies targets up to 170,000 GPUs in a 360MW facility in Batam, Indonesia, the second AI Compute Partnership adopter. ‖ e4: The revenue-share percentage under the program was not disclosed, and Nvidia declined further detail beyond its blog post co-authored by CFO Colette Kress. ‖ e5: Earlier Nvidia demand guarantees and referrals supported CoreWeave's $6.3 billion and Lambda's $1.5 billion of debt raises. ‖ e7: Sharon AI partnered with VAST Data for 600PB of storage supporting roughly 100,000 GPUs across its sovereign cloud operations.

## gnews:a8fab49de53d125314a6688a · call 139
- luna-1 unmatched (8): e1: The article reports that Nvidia's new AI data centers are 100% liquid-cooled and run at 45°C. ‖ e2: Nvidia, in a blog post by sustainability head Josh Parker, said its DSX AI factories are hosting Rubin AI infrastructure without fans or cold aisles. ‖ e3: The article describes Nvidia's DSX cooling design as taking coolant into the chip at 45°C and out at 55°C. ‖ e4: The article says processors can operate at full performance at the stated temperature without degrading. ‖ e5: The article claims that each 1°F increase in operating temperature can save up to 5% in energy costs for data centers. ‖ e6: The article reports data center water consumption of 5 million gallons per day, without identifying the source or population covered. ‖ e7: Nvidia claims its DSX AI factories use zero water through a dry-cooler, closed-loop design without evaporative cooling. ‖ e8: Josh Parker said AI compute demand driving data center construction is growing faster than almost any other category of infrastructure investment; no quantified period or amount was given.
- luna-2 unmatched (8): e1: The article reports that NVIDIA’s new AI data centers are 100% liquid-cooled and run at 45°C. ‖ e2: NVIDIA’s sustainability head Josh Parker announced that DSX AI factories are hosting Rubin AI infrastructure without fans or cold aisles. ‖ e3: The article describes NVIDIA’s DSX design as bringing coolant to the chip at 45°C and releasing it at 55°C. ‖ e4: The article says processors can operate at full performance at the described temperature without degradation. ‖ e5: The article claims that each 1°F increase in operating temperature can save up to 5% in energy costs. ‖ e6: The article reports that data centers consume 5 million gallons of water per day; it does not identify the source or population behind the figure. ‖ e7: NVIDIA claims its DSX AI factories use zero water through a dry-cooler, closed-loop design without evaporative cooling. ‖ e8: Josh Parker said AI workloads are not getting lighter and compute demand driving data center construction is growing faster than almost any other infrastructure-investment category.
- luna-3 unmatched (7): e1: The article reports that NVIDIA's new AI data centers run at 45°C and are 100% liquid-cooled; no period is specified. ‖ e2: NVIDIA's blog post said DSX AI factories host Rubin AI infrastructure without fans or cold aisles; no date or deployment quantity is specified. ‖ e3: The article describes NVIDIA DSX cooling with coolant entering at 45°C and exiting at 55°C; no period is specified. ‖ e4: The article says each 1°F increase in operating temperature can yield up to 5% energy-cost savings; no period or named source is given. ‖ e5: The article says data centers consume a reported 5 million gallons of water per day; it does not name the source or population covered. ‖ e6: NVIDIA claims its DSX AI factories use zero water through a dry-cooler-based closed-loop design without evaporative cooling; no period or deployment quantity is specified. ‖ e7: Josh Parker said AI compute demand driving data center construction is growing faster than almost any other infrastructure-investment category; he gave no quantified period or rate.
- haiku-1 unmatched (5): e1: Nvidia says its new 100% liquid-cooled AI data centers run at 45°C (113°F), far above the 18–27°C traditionally recommended. ‖ e2: Nvidia announced that its DSX AI factories are hosting Rubin AI infrastructure with no fans or cold aisles. ‖ e3: Coolant enters the chip at 45°C and exits at 55°C in the DSX design, per the article's account of Nvidia's design. ‖ e4: Nvidia claims its DSX AI factories have zero water consumption thanks to a closed-loop dry-cooler design. ‖ e5: AI data centers are reportedly consuming 5 million gallons of water per day; the figure is unattributed.

## gnews:c1658c3cc3b216fa2b0eaa54 · call 140
- luna-1 unmatched (10): e1: Valve is reported to be actively working to bring SteamOS support to Nvidia GPUs; no release date is specified here. ‖ e2: XDA Developers reported the Steam Machine price as $1,049. ‖ e3: Valve engineer Pierre-Loup Griffais said Valve is working with AMD, Intel, and Nvidia to improve SteamOS across a wider range of hardware. ‖ e4: Pierre-Loup Griffais said in a Bluesky post that additional GPU support was being worked on. ‖ e5: Valve updated the SteamOS installation image to version 3.8.10, expanding support to more AMD-powered handhelds and AMD discrete GPUs. ‖ e6: Pierre-Loup Griffais said he did not expect Nvidia SteamOS support before 2027. ‖ e7: XDA Developers stated that over 90% of PC gamers use Nvidia hardware. ‖ e8: XDA Developers reported that official SteamOS support covered the Steam Deck, Steam Machine, and Lenovo Legion Go S. ‖ e9: XDA Developers reported that Valve raised the Steam Machine target price from $749 to at least $1,049. ‖ e10: An unnamed commenter claimed Nvidia had acknowledged willingness to make its drivers more open and that Valve would play a key role.
- luna-2 unmatched (6): e1: Valve engineer Pierre-Loup Griffais said Valve is working with AMD, Intel, and Nvidia to improve SteamOS on a wider range of hardware. ‖ e2: Pierre-Loup Griffais said SteamOS GPU support beyond AMD was being worked on; he also said users with AMD GPUs could build their own Steam Machine. ‖ e3: Valve's SteamOS 3.8.10 installation image added support for more AMD-powered handhelds and AMD discrete GPUs. ‖ e4: Pierre-Loup Griffais said he did not expect Nvidia SteamOS support before 2027. ‖ e5: XDA Developers reported that Valve raised the Steam Machine's target price from $749 to at least $1,049. ‖ e6: XDA Developers reported that the Steam Deck, Steam Machine, and Lenovo Legion Go S were the only devices with official SteamOS support at publication.
- luna-3 unmatched (5): e1: Valve engineer Pierre-Loup Griffais said Valve is working with AMD, Intel, and Nvidia to improve SteamOS on a wider range of hardware; no completion date was specified. ‖ e2: Pierre-Loup Griffais did not expect Nvidia support for SteamOS before 2027. ‖ e3: Valve's SteamOS installation image version 3.8.10 expanded support to additional AMD-powered handhelds and AMD discrete GPUs; the update date is not stated. ‖ e4: The article reported that Valve raised the Steam Machine's $749 target price to at least $1,049; the date of the change is not stated. ‖ e5: The article reported that, as of 2026-07-04, the Steam Deck, Steam Machine, and Lenovo Legion Go S were the only devices with official SteamOS support.
- haiku-1 unmatched (6): e1 [refused]: Valve engineer Pierre-Loup Griffais said Valve is working with AMD, Intel and Nvidia to improve SteamOS on a wider range of hardware. ‖ e2: Griffais does not expect official SteamOS support for Nvidia GPUs before 2027. ‖ e3: Steam Machine price was raised from a $749 target to at least $1,049. ‖ e4: Valve's SteamOS 3.8.10 installation image expanded support to more AMD-powered handhelds and AMD discrete GPUs. ‖ e5: Only the Steam Deck, Steam Machine and Lenovo Legion Go S officially support SteamOS. ‖ e6: Author states that over 90% of PC gamers using Nvidia hardware feel like second-class citizens on SteamOS.

- luna-1 unmatched (14): e2: Alan Wei Zhaolun pleaded not guilty in a local court on Monday; his lawyer described the charges as "misconceived". ‖ e3: Alan Wei was released on bail after the bail amount was raised by $450,000 to more than $1.2 million on Monday, according to his lawyers. ‖ e4: Prosecutors alleged that Wei defrauded major server makers to obtain hardware that may have contained Nvidia AI processors subject to US export controls. ‖ e5: Singaporean prosecutors pressed fraud-by-false-representation charges against Wei. ‖ e6: Prosecutors have not accused Nvidia, Dell, Super Micro or Asustek of wrongdoing in the case and are focusing on individuals at Aperia and another Chinese citizen. ‖ e7: A police statement published July 1 alleged that Wei, Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim falsely told server suppliers between November 2023 and February 2025 that an Aperia Group company would be the servers' end-user. ‖ e8: Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim have also been charged with fraud. ‖ e9: Aperia said on Monday that companies in its group and Wei were fully cooperating with the authorities. ‖ e10: Aperia said on Monday that its companies deny the allegations of wrongdoing. ‖ e12: Prosecutors alleged that more than half of the funds used to buy Wei's $55 million mansion came from his criminal conduct. ‖ e13: Singaporean authorities blocked the sale or transfer of Wei's mansion. ‖ e14: Local authorities alleged that Li made fraudulent representations to Super Micro on two occasions in late 2023. ‖ e15: Li allegedly told Super Micro that he was an employee of Luxuriate Your Life, a Singapore-based company he controls. ‖ e16: Local police alleged that Li told Super Micro Luxuriate Your Life would retain servers it bought and lease them to other companies.
- luna-2 unmatched (12): e2: Alan Wei Zhaolun pleaded not guilty in court on Monday; his lawyer called the charges “misconceived.” ‖ e3: Wei’s lawyers said his bail amount was raised by $450,000 to more than $1.2 million on July 6. ‖ e4: Prosecutors alleged that Wei defrauded server makers to access hardware that may have contained Nvidia AI processors subject to US export controls; no amount or period is specified. ‖ e5: Prosecutors also charged Wei with fraud by false representation; the article gives no filing date. ‖ e6: A police statement published July 1 said that between November 2023 and February 2025, three Aperia executives—Wei, Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim—falsely identified an Aperia Group company as the servers’ end-user to suppliers. ‖ e7: Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim were charged with fraud; the article gives no charge date. ‖ e8: Aperia said on Monday that group companies and Wei were fully cooperating with authorities and that the companies denied the allegations. ‖ e10: Prosecutors alleged that more than half of the funds for Wei’s $55 million mansion came from criminal conduct; the article gives no amount or purchase date for those funds. ‖ e11: Singaporean local authorities blocked the sale or transfer of Wei’s mansion; the article gives no date for the action. ‖ e12: Singaporean local authorities alleged that Li made two fraudulent representations to Super Micro in late 2023. ‖ e13: Local authorities alleged that Li, who controls Luxuriate Your Life, told Super Micro once that he was an employee of that company; the article does not specify the date. ‖ e14: Singapore police said Li allegedly told Super Micro that Luxuriate Your Life would retain purchased servers and lease them to other companies; the article gives no date or quantity.

reported July 5, 2026. ‖ e10: Aperia said its group companies and Wei were fully cooperating with authorities in a statement on July 6, 2026. ‖ e11: Aperia said on July 6, 2026, that its companies faced allegations of wrongdoing and denied them. ‖ e13: Prosecutors alleged that more than half of the funds for Wei’s $55 million mansion purchase came from criminal conduct. ‖ e14: Local authorities blocked the sale or transfer of Wei’s mansion; ## nvblog:nations-deploy-ai-strategic-priorities · call 197
- luna-1 unmatched (7): e1: NVIDIA says its AI Nations initiative has helped countries in every region build AI ecosystems and workforces since 2019. ‖ e2: NVIDIA says ThinkDeep AI agents built on its platform are helping France’s Ministry of Economy and Finance process millions of documents and data sources for public-service workflows. ‖ e3 [refused]: NVIDIA says the ThinkDeep deployment cut document search times at France’s Ministry of Economy and Finance from two days to two minutes. ‖ e4 [refused]: NVIDIA says the ThinkDeep deployment saved 2 million euros for 10,000 employees at France’s Ministry of Economy and Finance. ‖ e5: NVIDIA says India’s Sarvam platform, powered by NVIDIA GPUs and built on domestic infrastructure, provides AI models and voice agents optimized for 22 official languages. ‖ e6 [refused]: NVIDIA says India’s Sarvam platform enables government and enterprise services to reach hundreds of millions of people in their own languages. ‖ e7: NVIDIA says Widelabs AI solutions on NVIDIA-accelerated infrastructure are helping Brazil’s Public Ministry of Rio Grande do Sul expand legal-service access for more than 8 million citizens across nearly 500 municipalities.

## nvblog:open-models-icml-2026 · call 200
- luna-1 unmatched (12): e1: NVIDIA said 74 of its papers were accepted at ICML 2026. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said approximately 2,000 accepted ICML 2026 papers cite NVIDIA GPUs. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said 145 accepted ICML 2026 papers cite Nemotron and its open datasets as a foundation for research. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said hundreds more accepted ICML 2026 papers draw on Cosmos, Isaac GR00T, BioNeMo and other NVIDIA open model families. ‖ e5: Basecamp Research developed EDEN, a DNA foundation model for interpreting and designing genetic sequences. ‖ e6: NVIDIA reported that Merck uses the KERMT model to predict potential drug molecules' behavior in the body. ‖ e7: Sakana AI built its Fugu and Fugu-Ultra models on Nemotron 3 Ultra. ‖ e8: KiloCode integrated Nemotron into its code-routing architecture and reported token cost reductions of up to 90%. ‖ e9: NAVER developed a model using the Nemotron architecture for Korean-language AI research. ‖ e10: Together AI is hosting Nemotron models on its platform. ‖ e11: NVIDIA said Humanoid, LG Electronics, NEURA Robotics and Noble Machines are adopting Isaac GR00T models for humanoid deployments. ‖ e12: NVIDIA said 1X, Agility, Agile Robots, Boston Dynamics, Hexagon Robotics and Mentee are building humanoids using Cosmos world models, Isaac Sim and Isaac Lab.

markets. ‖ e6: SemiAnalysis estimates AI debt financing will grow from hundreds of billions of dollars in 2024 and 2025 to about $7.1 trillion by 2029. ‖ e7 [refused]: SemiAnalysis says Nvidia’s GPU-rental backstop program typically runs for six years. ‖ e8: SemiAnalysis’s illustrative lower-end backstop schedule averages $2.36 per GPU-hour over six years. ‖ e9: SemiAnalysis models GB300 one-year rental pricing starting at $6.75 per GPU-hour in year one. ‖ e10: SemiAnalysis cites about $4.00 per GPU-hour as the current six-year fixed rental price in its modeled comparison. ‖ e11: In SemiAnalysis’s 40%-revenue-share example, year-one GB300 rental at $6.75/hour against a $3.68/hour backstop yields Nvidia $1.23/hour and the Neocloud $5.52/hour total realized revenue per GPU-hour. ‖ e12: SemiAnalysis models Nvidia’s average take rate at about 18% over six years in its short-term rental scenario. ‖ e13: SemiAnalysis models a 25.4% IRR for the one-year rental-book scenario, the financing. ‖ e18: SemiAnalysis models PBT margin falling from 14.8% to 5.4% as funding cost rises from 5.62% to 10%. ‖ e19: SemiAnalysis says lenders target at least 1.3x DSCR in early years of Nvidia-backstopped GPU loans, typically corresponding to 70%–80% LTV. ‖ e20: SemiAnalysis estimates current five-year hyperscaler-backstopped deals at roughly SOFR plus 225 basis points, a 195-basis-point Z-spread or about 5.9% yield. ‖ e21: SemiAnalysis cites an approximately 10% yield and 600-basis-point Z-spread for CoreWeave’s five-year unsecured corporate bond. ‖ e24: SharonAI says its footprint is planned to reach 132 MW, including 102 MW contracted, and more than 55,000 Nvidia GPUs by mid-2027, including the 40,000 backstopped GB300s. ‖ e26: Firmus launched an 18,000-GB300 cluster in a 42 MW self-built Melbourne datacenter. ‖ e27: Firmus’s Melbourne project was financed through a $10 billion facility led by Blackstone and supported by Coatue. ‖ e29: Firmus announced a 600 MW firm-energy deal with Gunvor supporting 1.2 GW of new renewables development and 1.5 GWh of storage in South Australia by 2032. ‖ e31: SemiAnalysis reported in June 2025 that AMD offered AWS, OCI, DigitalOcean, Vultr, Tensorwave, Crusoe and other neoclouds long-term rental backstops for unsold capacity in exchange for commitments to buy more AMD GPUs. ‖ e33 [refused]: SemiAnalysis says Nvidia has begun offering support by backstopping neocloud GPU-rental offtakes. ‖ e35: SemiAnalysis says multiple neoclouds are negotiating with Nvidia over its backstop program, while others are considering participation.

- luna-1 unmatched (15): e2: Polling cited in Tom’s Hardware reporting found that 7 out of 10 Americans did not want a data center built near their home. ‖ e3: On July 1, 2026, Valar Atomics and Nvidia demonstrated a Blackwell-based Nvidia desktop unit powered by Valar’s Ward 250 microreactor, served a website using its electricity, and announced a plan for a larger project. ‖ e4: Valar Atomics said Ward 250 operated at 37% of intended output during the July 1, 2026 demonstration; CEO Isaiah Taylor described that output as roughly 100 kilowatts of thermal energy. ‖ e5: Isaiah Taylor said during the July 1, 2026 event that reactor-generated electricity was powering an Nvidia Blackwell chip serving the website. ‖ e6: Bloomberg reported that the Utah demonstration was the first time a next-generation reactor in the US produced electricity to run an AI chip. ‖ e7: Valar Atomics’ Ward 250 was flown to Utah aboard Air Force C-17s in February 2026 and went critical on June 18, 2026. ‖ e8: Ward 250 operates under a Department of Energy authorization for research and demonstration, not a Nuclear Regulatory Commission commercial license. ‖ e9: Reuters reported that Valar Atomics joined Texas and Utah in litigation arguing that the NRC lacks licensing authority over some microreactors. ‖ e10: Valar Atomics and Nvidia announced an agreement to jointly explore nuclear-powered AI systems on July 1, 2026. ‖ e12: Nvidia said its DSX facility design reduces cooling-water use from roughly 2.6 million gallons per megawatt per year to near zero. ‖ e14: Utah Governor Spencer Cox issued an executive order in spring 2026 establishing guardrails for data center development, amid debate over Kevin O’Leary’s Stratos project in Box Elder County. ‖ e15: Utah Office of Energy Development director Emy Lesofski told FOX 13 that the state would not allow data center construction unless resources were used responsibly. ‖ e16: A 9-gigawatt data center campus was rising along Utah’s I-15 corridor; a state physicist compared its heat output to 23 atom bombs per day. ‖ e17: The Department of Energy reported that Antares went critical in early June 2026, Valar’s reactor on June 18, and Deployable Energy’s Unity on June 30—three new reactor designs reaching criticality in one month. ‖ e18: Isaiah Taylor said in the Department of Energy’s criticality announcement that the site had been empty nine months earlier.
- luna-2 unmatched (19): e2: Polling cited in Tom’s Hardware reporting found that 7 out of 10 Americans did not want a data center built near their home. ‖ e3: Valar Atomics and Nvidia staged an event in Utah on July 1, 2026. ‖ e4: At the July 1, 2026 event, Valar Atomics connected an Nvidia Blackwell-based desktop unit to its Ward 250 microreactor, served a website using its electricity, and announced a plan for a larger project designed to use no local water. ‖ e5: Valar Atomics’ Ward 250 is a TRISO-fueled high-temperature gas reactor cooled with pressurized helium. ‖ e6: Valar Atomics said Ward 250 was running at 37% of intended output during the July 1 show; CEO Isaiah Taylor described that output as roughly 100 kilowatts thermal. ‖ e7: Isaiah Taylor said at the July 1, 2026 event that Ward 250’s electricity was powering an Nvidia Blackwell chip serving the website. ‖ e8: Bloomberg reported that the Utah demonstration was the first time a next-generation reactor in the U.S. had produced power to run an AI chip. ‖ e9: Valar Atomics’ Ward 250 was flown to Utah aboard Air Force C-17s in February 2026 and went critical on June 18, 2026. ‖ e10: Ward 250 operates under a Department of Energy authorization for research and demonstration, not a Nuclear Regulatory Commission commercial license. ‖ e11: Reuters reported that Valar Atomics joined Texas and Utah in litigation arguing that the NRC lacks licensing authority over some microreactors. ‖ e12: Valar Atomics and Nvidia announced an agreement on July 1, 2026, to jointly explore nuclear-powered AI systems. ‖ e14: Nvidia said its DSX facility design reduces cooling-water use from roughly 2.6 million gallons per megawatt per year to near zero by using warm-water loops instead of conventional chillers. ‖ e16: FOX 13 reported that the proposed Emery County factory had no timeline as of the July 1, 2026 event. ‖ e17: Utah Governor Spencer Cox issued an executive order in spring 2026 putting guardrails on data center development, amid public opposition to Kevin O’Leary’s Stratos project in Box Elder County. ‖ e18: Utah Office of Energy Development head Emy Lesofski told FOX 13 that Utah would not allow data center construction unless resources were used responsibly. ‖ e19: A 9-gigawatt data center campus was rising along Utah’s I-15 corridor; a state physicist compared its heat to 23 atom bombs per day. ‖ e20: The Department of Energy reported that Antares went critical in early June 2026, Valar Atomics’ reactor on June 18, and Deployable Energy’s Unity on June 30, bringing three new reactor designs to criticality in one month. ‖ e21: Isaiah Taylor said in the Department of Energy’s criticality announcement that the site had been empty nine months earlier. ‖ e22: Valar Atomics’ stated goal of producing power by July 4, 2026, was achieved on July 1, 2026.

- luna-1 unmatched (12): e2: As of July 6, 2026, Alan Wei Zhaolun faced 11 charges in total. ‖ e3: The charges allege Alan Wei used more than $38 million in ill-gotten gains to buy a house between July and October 2024. ‖ e4: Singapore police said the bungalow was valued at around $55 million; the statement date is not specified in the item. ‖ e5: The charges allege Wei acquired more than $5 million in total in two bank accounts in 2024. ‖ e6: The charges allege around $3.2 million of the bank-account amount was benefit from purported criminal conduct. ‖ e7: The charges allege Wei conspired with Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim to commit fraud multiple times in 2023 and 2024. ‖ e8: Singapore police said the fraud charges allege a conspiracy involving server purchases from Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus. ‖ e9: A Singapore police spokesperson said the charges allege the defendants falsely told suppliers an Aperia Group company would be the end-user of the servers. ‖ e10: Singapore authorities charged Aperia International with five counts of fraud and Aperia Cloud Services (II) with two counts on July 6, 2026. ‖ e11: Singapore authorities charged A-Speed Infotech with one count of fraud on July 6, 2026. ‖ e12: Lawyers for Wei and the companies said on July 6, 2026, that their clients would contest all charges. ‖ e13: Wei, Aaron Woon and Li Ming were first brought to court in February 2025.
- luna-2 unmatched (13): e2: Alan Wei Zhaolun faced 11 charges in total as of July 6, 2026. ‖ e3: The charges accuse Alan Wei Zhaolun of using more than $38 million in alleged ill-gotten gains to buy a house between July and October 2024. ‖ e4: Singapore police said the bungalow was valued at around $55 million; the statement date is not specified in the item. ‖ e5: The charges reportedly allege that Alan Wei Zhaolun acquired more than $5 million in two bank accounts in 2024. ‖ e6: Around $3.2 million of the amount in the two accounts was alleged to be proceeds of criminal conduct. ‖ e7: The latest charges allege that Alan Wei Zhaolun conspired with Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim to commit fraud multiple times in 2023 and 2024. ‖ e8: Singapore police said fraud charges allege a conspiracy linked to server purchases from Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus. ‖ e9: A police spokesperson said the charges alleged that the defendants falsely represented to the server suppliers that an Aperia Group company would be the end-user. ‖ e10: Aperia International was charged with five counts of fraud and Aperia Cloud Services (II) with two counts on July 6, 2026. ‖ e11: A-Speed Infotech was charged with one count of fraud on July 6, 2026. ‖ e12: Lawyers for Wei and the companies said on July 6, 2026, that they would contest all charges. ‖ e13: Wei's lawyers said the companies and Wei were cooperating with authorities and wanted regulatory changes considered in reviewing the alleged offences. ‖ e14: Wei, Woon and Li Ming first appeared in court and received fraud-related charges in February 2025, after a US investigation examined whether DeepSeek had evaded US restrictions on advanced Nvidia chips by buying through third countries.
- luna-3 unmatched (15): e2: Alan Wei Zhaolun faced 11 charges in total as of July 6, 2026. ‖ e3: Prosecutors accused Wei of using more than $38 million in alleged ill-gotten gains to buy a house between July and October 2024. ‖ e4: Singapore police earlier said the bungalow was valued at around $55 million. ‖ e5: Wei allegedly acquired more than $5 million in two bank accounts in 2024. ‖ e6: Around $3.2 million of the alleged 2024 bank-account amount was said to be proceeds of purported criminal conduct. ‖ e7: The latest charges alleged that Wei conspired with Aaron Woon Guo Jie and Jenny Lim to commit fraud multiple times in 2023 and 2024. ‖ e8: Singapore police said fraud charges allege that Wei, Woon and Lim conspired in connection with server purchases from Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus. ‖ e9: A police spokesperson said the charges allege that the trio falsely represented to suppliers that an Aperia Group company would be the servers’ end-user. ‖ e10: Aperia International was charged with five fraud counts and Aperia Cloud Services (II) with two on July 6, 2026. ‖ e11: A-Speed Infotech was charged with one fraud count on July 6, 2026. ‖ e12: Wei’s lawyers said on July 6, 2026, that Wei and the companies would contest all charges. ‖ e13: Wei’s lawyers said the companies and Wei were cooperating with authorities and wanted regulatory changes considered in reviewing the alleged offences. ‖ e14: Wei’s lawyers said the charges concerned transactions involving suppliers and customers from the United States and elsewhere that required compliance across multiple jurisdictions. ‖ e15: Wei, Woon and Li Ming first appeared in court in February 2025. ‖ e16: A US investigation examined whether DeepSeek circumvented US restrictions on advanced Nvidia chips by buying chips through third parties in countries including Singapore.
- haiku-1 unmatched (18): e2: Wei now faces 11 charges in total after the July 6 additional charges. ‖ e3: Wei is accused of using over $38 million of alleged ill-gotten gains to buy a house in Chee Hoon Avenue between July and October 2024. ‖ e4: Police earlier stated the Chee Hoon Avenue bungalow was valued at around $55 million. ‖ e5: About $3.2 million of the amount is alleged to be benefits of criminal conduct. ‖ e6: Wei is said to have acquired over $5 million in total across two bank accounts in 2024. ‖ e7: Three Wei-linked companies were charged with fraud on July 6. ‖ e8: Aperia International faces five fraud counts and Aperia Cloud Services (II) two, as of July 6. ‖ e9: A-Speed Infotech faces one fraud count. ‖ e10: Wei allegedly conspired with Aaron Woon and Jenny Lim to commit fraud multiple times in 2023 and 2024. ‖ e11: Fraud charges allege a trio conspired over purchases of servers from Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus. ‖ e12: Police say the defendants falsely told suppliers that an Aperia Group company would be the end-user of the servers. ‖ e13: Pre-trial conferences for Wei and the three companies are scheduled for Aug 14. ‖ e14: Wei's lawyers say the companies and Wei are fully cooperating with authorities. ‖ e15: Wei's lawyers say Wei and the companies will contest all charges. ‖ e16: Lawyers say the charges relate to transactions with US and other suppliers and customers requiring multi-jurisdictional compliance. ‖ e17: Wei, Woon and Li Ming first appeared in court in February 2025. ‖ e18: A US investigation examined whether DeepSeek circumvented US restrictions on advanced Nvidia chips via third parties, including in Singapore. ‖ e19: Jenny Lim was charged later and the cases are pending.

## gnews:4249e0578b11cedf5a71ff08 · call 355
- luna-1 unmatched (13): e1: NVIDIA said ML-based beamforming delivered up to 1.62x the throughput of traditional zero-forcing in 64T64R massive MIMO scenarios; no period was specified. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said deep reinforcement learning link adaptation achieved 1.3x the cell-edge throughput of outer-loop link adaptation while keeping latency below 30 microseconds; no period was specified. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said academic research reported a 20% spectral-efficiency improvement from pilotless spatial multiplexing and up to 70% higher throughput from practical DRL link adaptation in high-mobility conditions; no period was specified. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said it is collaborating with Nokia on AI-RAN platforms integrated with Nokia anyRAN software for AI-native 5G-Advanced networks intended to be ready for 6G evolution; no timetable was given. ‖ e5: NVIDIA said U.S. telecom operators spent more than $240 billion acquiring wireless spectrum over the prior 30 years. ‖ e6: NVIDIA analysis said AI beamforming produced up to 1.62x the throughput of traditional zero-forcing at 32 layers in a 64T64R MU-MIMO scenario with 16 users; users had randomly assigned SNRs of 0–20 dB. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said ML beamforming delivered a 1.28x throughput improvement at a lower number of layers; the comparison baseline and period were not specified. ‖ e8: SoftBank and NVIDIA reported stable outdoor 16-layer massive MU-MIMO operation on a GPU-based AI-RAN platform, with roughly 3x the spectral efficiency of a conventional 4-layer baseline; the trial date was not specified. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said early engineering results showed a 1.3x cell-edge throughput gain over baseline OLLA when combined with channel-orthogonality-based user pairing; no period was specified. ‖ e10: NVIDIA said its GPU-based architecture stayed below an approximately 30-microsecond reference latency budget across the tested user range, including with a 396,000-parameter model. ‖ e11: NVIDIA summarized academic research as finding a 20% improvement in spectral efficiency; the study period and specific conditions were not stated in this summary. ‖ e12: NVIDIA summarized academic research as finding a 67% block error rate reduction versus OLLA in a URLLC scenario; the study period was not specified. ‖ e13: NVIDIA summarized academic research as finding up to 70% higher throughput than classical OLLA in a high-mobility scenario; the study period was not specified.
