- luna-1 unmatched (8): e1: NVIDIA said Olympus delivers 50% higher instructions per cycle than NVIDIA Grace. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said Vera supports up to 1.2 TB/s of LPDDR5X memory bandwidth at less than 40 watts of memory power and 3.4 TB/s of core-to-core bandwidth, which it said is 3x greater than any other data center CPU. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said Vera has 88 cores, each with access to the CPU's full memory performance. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said Vera delivers 1.8x the sustained per-core performance of x86 in loaded CPU workloads representing agentic execution. ‖ e5: NVIDIA said Perplexity tested Vera on a coding workflow and found it completed the job about 1.5x faster than x86 and started concurrent sandboxes up to 1.9x faster. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said partners measured 3x faster large-scale SQL analytics with Starburst and up to 6x lower real-time streaming latency with Redpanda, both versus leading x86 server CPUs. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said its next-generation Rosa CPU with the Rigel core will continue its CPU roadmap for the agentic AI era; no launch date was specified. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said its Rigel CPU core will deliver higher per-core performance than Olympus while keeping the same silicon footprint.
- luna-2 unmatched (9): e1: NVIDIA said Olympus, Vera’s CPU core, delivers 50% higher instructions per cycle than NVIDIA Grace. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said Vera supports up to 1.2 TB/s of LPDDR5X memory bandwidth at under 40 watts of memory power, and 3.4 TB/s of core-to-core bandwidth, three times that of any other data center CPU. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said Vera’s 88 cores can access the CPU’s full memory performance without bottlenecks. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said Vera delivers 1.8 times the sustained per-core performance of x86 in loaded CPU workloads representing agentic execution. ‖ e5: NVIDIA reported that Perplexity tested Vera and found it completed a coding workflow about 1.5 times faster than x86 and started concurrent sandboxes up to 1.9 times faster. ‖ e7: NVIDIA reported partner measurements of three times faster large-scale SQL analytics with Starburst and up to six times lower real-time streaming latency with Redpanda, versus leading x86 server CPUs. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said its next-generation Rosa CPU, with the Rigel core, will continue its CPU roadmap for the agentic AI era. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said its future Rigel core will deliver higher per-core performance than Olympus while keeping the same silicon footprint. ‖ e10: NVIDIA forecast that there will be billions of agents in the agentic AI era, each using a CPU for agent tasks.
- luna-3 unmatched (7): e1: NVIDIA said on 2026-07-07 that Vera’s Olympus core delivers 50% higher instructions per cycle than Grace. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said on 2026-07-07 that Vera provides up to 1.2 TB/s of LPDDR5X memory bandwidth at less than 40 watts of memory power, and 3.4 TB/s of core-to-core bandwidth, 3x that of any other data-center CPU. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said on 2026-07-07 that Vera’s 88 cores can access the CPU’s full memory performance without bottlenecks. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said on 2026-07-07 that Vera delivers 1.8x the sustained per-core performance of x86 in loaded CPU workloads representing agentic execution. ‖ e5: NVIDIA reported on 2026-07-07 that Perplexity’s coding workflow completed about 1.5x faster on Vera than on x86, and concurrent sandboxes started up to 1.9x faster. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said on 2026-07-07 that Starburst measured 3x faster large-scale SQL analytics and Redpanda measured up to 6x lower real-time streaming latency on Vera versus leading x86 server CPUs. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said on 2026-07-07 that its next-generation Rigel Arm v9.2 CPU core is designed to deliver higher per-core performance than Olympus at the same silicon footprint.

## gnews:56085a9d3e88c464bcaed67b · call 357
- luna-1 unmatched (13): e1: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 was released under the Apache 2.0 license with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint. ‖ e2: NVIDIA reported GR00T 1.7 benchmark improvements over N1.6 of 10% on DROID-F0, 61% on DROID-F6, 5% on SimplerEnv Bridge, and 2% on Fractal. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said its platform was being adopted by 1X, Agility, ANYBotics, Skild AI, Stanford, ETH Zurich, HTC Vive, and PICO. ‖ e4: NVIDIA described the Isaac GR00T platform as supporting humanoid data collection and simulation-based training for developing, validating, and deploying policies on real robots. ‖ e5: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on approximately 32,000 hours of real demonstration and human egocentric data and approximately 8,000 hours of simulated rollouts and demonstrations. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 improved on N1.6 by 10% on DROID-F0 and 61% on DROID-F6. ‖ e7 [refused]: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 improved on N1.6 by 5% on SimplerEnv Bridge and 2% on Fractal. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said humanoid robotics partners were already adopting its GR00T platform and reference workflow. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said 1X, Agility, ANYBotics, Bellboy Robotics, FieldAI, Lightwheel AI, NEURA Robotics, Nexuni, Noble Machines, Schaeffler, Skild AI, and Techman Robot were integrating GR00T platform components. ‖ e10: NVIDIA said Stanford, CMU, UCSD, ETH Zurich, and AI2 would experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow. ‖ e11: NVIDIA said Haptikos, Manus, Noitom, OpenGraph Labs, PICO, Sensing, HTC Vive, Wuji, and Xsens offered devices with native support for Isaac Teleop. ‖ e12: NVIDIA said it collected 400 trajectories for the tutorial's pick-and-place task across multiple data-collection sessions. ‖ e13: NVIDIA's tutorial reported a 100% success rate and 100% object-moved rate in one evaluation episode.
- luna-2 unmatched (12): e1: NVIDIA released GR00T 1.7 under the Apache 2.0 license with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on approximately 32,000 hours of real data and 8,000 hours of simulated data. ‖ e3: NVIDIA reported that GR00T 1.7 improved the DROID-F0 benchmark by 10% versus N1.6. ‖ e4: NVIDIA reported that GR00T 1.7 improved the DROID-F6 benchmark by 61% versus N1.6. ‖ e5: NVIDIA reported that GR00T 1.7 improved the SimplerEnv Bridge benchmark by 5% versus N1.6. ‖ e6: NVIDIA reported that GR00T 1.7 improved the Fractal benchmark by 2% versus N1.6. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said it collected 400 trajectories across multiple sessions for the simulated pick-and-place task. ‖ e8: NVIDIA reported a 100% success rate and 100% object-moved rate in a one-episode simulation evaluation. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said 1X, Agility, ANYBotics, Bellboy Robotics, FieldAI, Lightwheel AI, NEURA Robotics, Nexuni, Noble Machines, Schaeffler, Skild AI, and Techman Robot are integrating GR00T platform components. ‖ e10: NVIDIA said Stanford, CMU, UCSD, ETH Zurich, and AI2 will experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow. ‖ e11: NVIDIA said Haptikos, Manus, Noitom, OpenGraph Labs, PICO, Sensing, HTC Vive, Wuji, and Xsens offer devices with native Isaac Teleop support. ‖ e12: NVIDIA said the GR00T platform and GR00T 1.7 model were available to developers on 2026-07-07.

- haiku-1 unmatched (10): e1: NVIDIA released GR00T 1.7, a vision-language-action model for humanoid skills, as open and commercially usable under Apache 2.0. ‖ e2: GR00T 1.7 model weights are publicly available with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint. ‖ e3: GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on about 32K hours of real and human ego-centric data plus about 8K hours of simulated data. ‖ e4: NVIDIA reports GR00T 1.7 benchmark gains over N1.6: DROID-F0 +10%, DROID-F6 +61%, SimplerEnv Bridge +5%, Fractal +2%. ‖ e5: NVIDIA says humanoid makers, research institutions and XR device makers, including 1X, Agility, Skild AI, Stanford and HTC Vive, are adopting the GR00T platform. ‖ e6: NVIDIA says twelve named humanoid makers and AI providers are integrating GR00T platform components. ‖ e7: NVIDIA says Stanford, CMU, UCSD, ETH Zurich and AI2 plan to experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow. ‖ e8: NVIDIA introduced the open-source Isaac GR00T Development Platform spanning the humanoid robot development pipeline. ‖ e9: NVIDIA collected 400 demonstration trajectories for its simulated pick-and-place example across multiple sessions. ‖ e10: NVIDIA states the GR00T platform and GR00T 1.7 model are available today to any developer.
- haiku-2 unmatched (8): e1: NVIDIA released GR00T 1.7, an open vision-language-action model under Apache 2.0 with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint, on 2026-07-07. ‖ e2: GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on about 32K hours of real demonstration and human ego-centric data and about 8K hours of simulated data. ‖ e3: NVIDIA reports GR00T 1.7 beats N1.6 on DROID-F0 by 10%, DROID-F6 by 61%, SimplerEnv Bridge by 5% and Fractal by 2%. ‖ e4: NVIDIA says humanoid makers 1X, Agility, ANYBotics and Skild AI, plus Stanford, ETH Zurich, HTC Vive and PICO, are adopting the GR00T platform. ‖ e5: NVIDIA says twelve humanoid makers and AI providers, including Schaeffler and Techman Robot, are integrating GR00T platform components. ‖ e6: NVIDIA says Stanford, CMU, UCSD, ETH Zurich and AI2 will experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow. ‖ e7: NVIDIA says nine wearable and XR device makers offer devices with native support for Isaac Teleop. ‖ e8: NVIDIA made GR00T 1.7 model weights publicly available on GitHub and Hugging Face.
- haiku-3 unmatched (8): e1: NVIDIA released GR00T 1.7, an open vision-language-action model under Apache 2.0 with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint. ‖ e2: GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on about 32K hours of real and human ego-centric data plus about 8K hours of simulated data. ‖ e3: NVIDIA reports GR00T 1.7 beats N1.6 on DROID-F0 by 10%, DROID-F6 by 61%, SimplerEnv Bridge by 5% and Fractal by 2%. ‖ e4: NVIDIA says humanoid makers, research institutions and XR device makers including 1X, Agility, Skild AI, Stanford and PICO are adopting the platform. ‖ e5: NVIDIA says twelve humanoid makers and AI providers are integrating GR00T platform components into their development pipelines. ‖ e6: NVIDIA says Stanford, CMU, UCSD, ETH Zurich and AI2 will experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow. ‖ e7: GR00T 1.7 model weights are publicly available via GitHub and Hugging Face. ‖ e8: NVIDIA says nine wearable and XR device makers offer devices with native support for Isaac Teleop.

## nvblog:hugging-face-lerobot-models-frameworks-open-robotics · call 358
- luna-1 unmatched (6): e1: NVIDIA and Hugging Face announced that they are bringing Isaac GR00T 1.7 and Isaac Teleop to LeRobot. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said Cosmos 3 is planned to come to LeRobot soon; no specific date was given. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said its partnership connects 3 million robotics developers with Hugging Face’s 16 million AI builders. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said its open-source physical AI dataset had been downloaded more than 15 million times and contains more than 350,000 trajectories and 57 million grasps. ‖ e5: NVIDIA described Isaac Lab-Arena as integrated in LeRobot Environment Hub and usable within the LeRobot ecosystem. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said Jetson Thor is integrated with LeRobot’s Reachy 2 to support VLA model deployment on open-source humanoid robots.

- luna-1 unmatched (23): e1: The U.S. Department of Defense designated Hesai a Chinese military entity and blacklisted it in 2024. ‖ e2: CNBC reported that the blacklist covered Hesai and 187 other companies and subsidiaries, barred them from Pentagon contracts, and did not prohibit nonmilitary use of their products. ‖ e3: Hesai and Nvidia expanded their partnership so automakers can choose Hesai sensors for integration into Nvidia autonomous-vehicle platforms; the item gives no start or end date. ‖ e4: Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said Nvidia was working toward a future in which every car and truck is autonomous; he gave no deadline or quantity for a period. ‖ e6 [refused]: Craig Singleton of the Foundation for Defense of Democracies said his research identified risks that Chinese-made sensors in U.S. systems could enable access to sensitive U.S. data or disruption of critical operations. ‖ e7: Craig Singleton said Chinese lidar sensors becoming more common in the U.S. would bring them closer to defense nodes, utility grids and airports. ‖ e8: Hesai disclosed in SEC filings that the Chinese government has significant oversight of its operations and may influence or intervene in them at any time. ‖ e9: Hesai CEO David Li said the company's sensors cannot hold data because they lack memory capacity. ‖ e10: Michael Robbins said a Hesai firmware update failed to account for leap year and all Hesai lidar sensors stopped working on February 29, 2024. ‖ e11: Hesai said it fixed the firmware issue within 24 hours. ‖ e12: Hesai told CNBC it held one-third of the global automotive lidar market; the statement does not specify a measurement date. ‖ e13: McKinsey estimated autonomous-driving market potential at roughly $300 billion to $400 billion by 2035. ‖ e14: Hesai told CNBC it reduced the per-unit cost of its lidar from more than $10,000 to less than $200; the item gives no dates for the compared prices. ‖ e15: Aeva told CNBC its automotive lidar sensors cost a few hundred dollars per unit; no date is specified. ‖ e16: Yole Group's 2025 automotive lidar report said Chinese firms such as Hesai were dominating on cost, scale and government support, while Western players faced higher costs and slower adoption. ‖ e17: Hesai's 2025 SEC annual filing disclosed Chinese government subsidies, a 15% preferential tax rate versus the standard 25%, below-benchmark borrowing rates, and an R&D deduction of 200% of costs. ‖ e18: Hesai said no government organization, including China's government, holds an equity stake in the company. ‖ e19: Hesai sued the U.S. Department of Defense over its designation in May 2024, after the department blacklisted Hesai in January 2024. ‖ e20: Hesai lost its lawsuit against the U.S. Department of Defense in 2025, remained blacklisted, and appealed the decision. ‖ e21: The Prague Security Studies Institute published a Hesai white paper in July 2025 reporting that a Hesai lidar sensor appeared on a military vehicle in a 2023 Chinese military television program. ‖ e22: David Li confirmed that the sensor shown in the military-program footage was Hesai technology, but said Hesai did not supply it for the competition. ‖ e24: U.S. Representative John Moolenaar introduced legislation in May to ban Chinese vehicles from U.S. roads; the item does not specify the year. ‖ e25: TP-Link Technologies was added to the Defense Department blacklist in June 2026.
- luna-2 unmatched (23): e1: The U.S. Department of Defense designated Hesai a Chinese military entity and placed it on its national-security blacklist in 2024. ‖ e2: Hesai and Nvidia expanded their partnership so automakers can select Hesai sensors for integration into Nvidia autonomous-vehicle platforms; the announcement was made in January 2026. ‖ e3: Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said in his January 2026 CES keynote that Nvidia was working toward a future in which every car and truck is autonomous. ‖ e5: Craig Singleton of the Foundation for Defense of Democracies said his research found Chinese-made sensors in U.S. systems could let Beijing access sensitive U.S. data or disrupt critical operations. ‖ e6: Craig Singleton told CNBC that increasing use of Chinese lidar sensors in the U.S. would bring them closer to defense nodes, utility grids and airports. ‖ e7: The SEC requires China-based companies to disclose risks of Chinese government intervention or control. ‖ e8: Hesai disclosed in SEC filings that the Chinese government has significant oversight of its operations and may influence or intervene at any time. ‖ e9: AUVSI CEO Michael Robbins said a Hesai firmware update failed to account for leap year and caused all Hesai lidar sensors to stop working on February 29, 2024. ‖ e10: Hesai said it fixed the February 2024 firmware issue within 24 hours. ‖ e11: Hesai told CNBC it held one-third of the global automotive lidar market; it is listed on Nasdaq and the Hong Kong Stock Exchange. ‖ e12: McKinsey estimated autonomous-driving market potential at roughly $300 billion to $400 billion by 2035. ‖ e13: Hesai told CNBC it reduced the price of its lidar units from more than $10,000 to less than $200 per unit. ‖ e14: Aeva told CNBC its automotive lidar sensors cost a few hundred dollars per unit. ‖ e15: Yole Group's 2025 automotive-lidar report said Chinese firms were dominating through cost, scale and government support, while Western firms faced higher costs and slower adoption. ‖ e16: Hesai's 2025 SEC annual filing disclosed Chinese government subsidies, a 15% preferential tax rate versus the standard 25%, below-benchmark borrowing rates, and a tax deduction of 200% of R&D costs. ‖ e17: Hesai sued the U.S. Department of Defense in federal court in May 2024 over its January 2024 blacklist designation. ‖ e18: Hesai lost its lawsuit against the U.S. Department of Defense in 2025, remained blacklisted, and appealed the ruling. ‖ e19: The Prague Security Studies Institute published a white paper on Hesai in July 2025. ‖ e20: The Prague Security Studies Institute's 2025 white paper said a Hesai lidar sensor appeared on a military vehicle in a 2023 Chinese military television program. ‖ e21: U.S. Representative John Moolenaar introduced legislation in May 2026 to ban Chinese vehicles from U.S. roads. ‖ e23: The FCC ruled in December 2025 that DJI could not sell new products to U.S. consumers on national-security grounds; existing models remained legal to use. ‖ e24: The FCC determined in March 2026 that foreign-made routers posed an unacceptable national-security risk and barred TP-Link Technologies from selling new models. ‖ e25: The U.S. Department of Defense added TP-Link Technologies to its blacklist in June 2026.

## gnews:2f1cb54b7a3da28d093523b6 · call 382
- luna-1 unmatched (14): e1: NVIDIA said the DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations expansion would launch later on 2026-07-07 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Ray Reconstruction and path tracing support. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced would launch on 2026-07-09 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Super Resolution and ray tracing. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said Gray Zone Warfare had upgraded to native DLSS 4.5 by 2026-07-07. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said the DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations expansion would launch on 2026-07-07 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation (up to 6X mode), Super Resolution and path tracing enhanced with Ray Reconstruction on supported GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e5: NVIDIA claimed the GeForce RTX 5090 could run DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at up to 310 frames per second in 4K with path tracing and maxed-out settings. ‖ e6: NVIDIA claimed 375 frames per second for the RTX 5090 and 280 frames per second for the RTX 5080 in DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at 2560x1440, and an overall 6.5X performance multiplier, using DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction. ‖ e7: NVIDIA claimed DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction accelerated frame rates by an average of 6.1X at 1920x1080 in DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations. ‖ e8: NVIDIA claimed at 1920x1080 that the RTX 5090 achieved 500 frames per second, the RTX 5080 exceeded 360, the RTX 5070 Ti surpassed 310 and the RTX 5070 ran at over 280 in DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations with maxed-out settings and path tracing. ‖ e9: NVIDIA announced a new DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations GeForce Game Ready Driver by 2026-07-07. ‖ e10: NVIDIA said the PC release of Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced would launch on 2026-07-09 with ray-traced reflections and lighting, and support for DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Frame Generation and Super Resolution on GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e11: NVIDIA said players could upgrade Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced through the NVIDIA app to DLSS 4.5 with DLSS Dynamic Multi Frame Generation and Super Resolution models. ‖ e12: NVIDIA said its newest GeForce Game Ready Driver included support for Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced ahead of its launch. ‖ e13: NVIDIA said Gray Zone Warfare had upgraded to Unreal Engine 5.5 and introduced new content in recent releases. ‖ e14: NVIDIA said the latest DLSS Dynamic Multi Frame Generation and Super Resolution models had been added to Gray Zone Warfare on GeForce RTX GPUs.

## gnews:c6445931955f107b551cce85 · call 395
### w395-x1 [exclude] The Singapore case and the bungalow: excluded (Alex, question 5).
- luna-1: kept e1 | fact | who=Singapore Police Force; the allegation is reported by e.vnexpress.net | day= | Singapore police seized a bungalow valued at S$55 million (US$42.5 million), allegedly bought with proceeds from a server-smuggling scheme involving chips restricted from export to China.
- luna-2: kept e1 | fact | who=Singapore police | day= | Singapore police seized a bungalow valued at S$55 million (US$42.5 million) in a case involving alleged server smuggling.
- luna-3: kept e1 | fact | who=Singapore Police Force | day=2026-07-07 | Singapore police seized a bungalow valued at S$55 million (US$42.5 million) in a case involving alleged server smuggling; the article says it was allegedly bought with scheme proceeds.
- haiku-1: —
- haiku-2: kept e1 | fact | who=Singapore Police Force | day=2026-07-07 | Singapore police seized a S$55M (US$42.5M) Good Class Bungalow at 12 Chee Hoon Avenue, allegedly bought with proceeds of a server-smuggling scheme.
- haiku-3: kept e1 | fact | who=Singapore Police Force | day=2026-07-07 | Singapore police seized a S$55 million (US$42.5 million) Good Class Bungalow allegedly bought with proceeds of a scheme to smuggle Nvidia-chip servers.
- luna-1 unmatched (16): e2: The Singapore Police Force issued a prohibition of disposal order on July 1, 2026, freezing the sale or transfer of the bungalow at 12 Chee Hoon Avenue. ‖ e3: Singapore officers seized about S$1 million from bank accounts tied to the case; Channel News Asia reported the seizure. ‖ e4: Aperia Group CEO Wei Zhaolun pleaded not guilty to 11 charges on July 6, 2026, and his bail was raised to S$1.25 million. ‖ e5: Wei's lawyer called the charges "misconceived." ‖ e6: Two money-laundering charges allege Wei received about S$5.1 million and S$698,712 in personal accounts during 2024, with some funds allegedly derived from criminal conduct. ‖ e7: A separate charge alleges Wei put roughly S$38 million in criminal proceeds toward the bungalow he bought in 2024. ‖ e8: Prosecutors allege Wei and two Aperia executives deceived Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus from November 2023 to February 2025 by certifying that Aperia-group companies would receive the servers. ‖ e9: Prosecutors identify Aperia CFO Jenny Lim and head of sales Aaron Woon Guo Jie as Wei's alleged co-conspirators. ‖ e10: Wei, Woon and Li Ming were first charged in February 2025; Lim was added in April 2026. ‖ e11: Li Ming faces fraud charges in the probe, while Wei's lawyers say Li's case is unconnected to Aperia. ‖ e12: Four companies—Aperia International, A-Speed Infotech, Aperia Cloud Services and Luxuriate Your Life—were charged in the case. ‖ e13: The Singapore Police Force said the four company charges were the first prosecution of corporate entities in the investigation. ‖ e14: An Aperia spokesperson told CNA that the companies were cooperating fully and denied wrongdoing. ‖ e15: Home Affairs and Law Minister K. Shanmugam said the servers were routed through Singapore and shipped to Malaysia; he made the statement when the first charges were laid in March 2025. ‖ e16: A Commerce Department inquiry examined whether DeepSeek obtained restricted Nvidia chips through intermediaries in Singapore and Malaysia. ‖ e17: Singapore police have not confirmed a link between Aperia and DeepSeek.
- luna-2 unmatched (16): e2: The Singapore Police Force issued an order on July 1, 2026, prohibiting the sale or transfer of the bungalow at 12 Chee Hoon Avenue. ‖ e3: Officers seized about S$1 million from bank accounts tied to the case; CNA reported the seizure. ‖ e4: Aperia Group CEO Wei Zhaolun pleaded not guilty to 11 charges on July 6, 2026, and his bail was raised to S$1.25 million. ‖ e5: Wei's lawyer called the charges against him “misconceived.” ‖ e6: Two money-laundering charges accuse Wei of receiving about S$5.1 million and S$698,712 in personal accounts during 2024. ‖ e7: A separate charge alleges Wei put roughly S$38 million in criminal proceeds toward buying the bungalow in 2024. ‖ e8: Prosecutors allege Wei and two Aperia executives deceived Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus from November 2023 to February 2025 with end-user forms stating that Aperia group companies would receive the servers. ‖ e9: Prosecutors identify Aperia CFO Jenny Lim and head of sales Aaron Woon Guo Jie as alleged co-conspirators in the case. ‖ e10: Wei, Woon and Li Ming were first charged in February 2025, and Jenny Lim was charged in April 2026. ‖ e11: Li Ming faces fraud charges in the probe; Wei's lawyers say Li's case is unconnected to Aperia. ‖ e12: Four companies—Aperia International, A-Speed Infotech, Aperia Cloud Services and Luxuriate Your Life—were charged in the investigation. ‖ e13: The Singapore Police Force said the four companies' prosecution was the first prosecution of corporate entities in the investigation. ‖ e14: An Aperia spokesperson said the companies were cooperating fully and denied wrongdoing. ‖ e15: Minister K. Shanmugam said the servers were routed through Singapore and shipped onward to Malaysia when the first charges were laid in March 2025. ‖ e16: The Diplomat reported a Commerce Department inquiry into whether DeepSeek obtained restricted Nvidia chips through intermediaries in Singapore and Malaysia. ‖ e17: Singapore police have not confirmed a link between Aperia and DeepSeek.
- luna-3 unmatched (16): e2: The Singapore Police Force issued a prohibition of disposal order on July 1, 2026, freezing sale or transfer of the bungalow at 12 Chee Hoon Avenue. ‖ e3: Singapore officers seized about S$1 million from bank accounts tied to the case, according to Channel News Asia. ‖ e4: Aperia Group CEO Wei Zhaolun pleaded not guilty to all 11 charges on July 6, 2026, and his bail was raised to S$1.25 million, Bloomberg reported. ‖ e5: Wei's lawyer described the charges as “misconceived.” ‖ e6: Two money laundering charges allege Wei received about S$5.1 million and S$698,712 in personal accounts during 2024, partly from alleged criminal conduct, according to charge sheets cited by Mothership. ‖ e7: A separate charge alleges Wei funneled roughly S$38 million in criminal proceeds into the bungalow, which he bought in 2024. ‖ e8: Prosecutors allege Wei and two Aperia executives deceived Dell, Super Micro Computer and Asus from November 2023 to February 2025 by certifying that Aperia-group companies would receive the servers. ‖ e9: Prosecutors identify Aperia CFO Jenny Lim and head of sales Aaron Woon Guo Jie as Wei's alleged co-conspirators. ‖ e10: Wei, Woon and Li Ming were first charged in February 2025; Lim was added in April 2026, according to the cited media reports. ‖ e11: Li Ming, who controls Luxuriate Your Life, faces fraud charges in the probe, according to the International Business Times; Wei's lawyers say Li's case is unconnected to Aperia. ‖ e12: Four companies—Aperia International, A-Speed Infotech, Aperia Cloud Services and Luxuriate Your Life—were charged in the case. ‖ e13: The Singapore Police Force said the investigation's charges marked the first time corporate entities had been prosecuted in it. ‖ e14: An Aperia spokesperson told CNA that the companies were cooperating fully and denied wrongdoing. ‖ e15: Home Affairs and Law Minister K. Shanmugam said the servers were routed through Singapore and shipped to Malaysia when the first charges were laid in March 2025. ‖ e16: The Diplomat reported that a U.S. Commerce Department inquiry investigated whether DeepSeek obtained restricted Nvidia chips through intermediaries in Singapore and Malaysia. ‖ e17: Singapore police had not confirmed a link between Aperia and DeepSeek as of the article's publication.
- haiku-1 unmatched (9): e1: Singapore police froze any sale or transfer of the S$55 million Good Class Bungalow at 12 Chee Hoon Avenue via a prohibition of disposal order issued July 1, 2026. ‖ e2: Officers seized about S$1 million from bank accounts tied to the Aperia case, as reported by Channel News Asia. ‖ e3: Aperia Group CEO Alan Wei pleaded not guilty on July 6, 2026 to all 11 charges and his bail was raised to S$1.25 million. ‖ e4: A charge alleges Wei funneled roughly S$38 million in criminal proceeds into the bungalow he bought in 2024. ‖ e5: Prosecutors allege Wei and two other Aperia executives deceived Dell, Super Micro and Asus from November 2023 to February 2025 by certifying end-user forms for server delivery. ‖ e6: Four companies, including Aperia International and Aperia Cloud Services, were charged, the first corporate prosecutions in the investigation. ‖ e7: Minister K. - luna-1 unmatched (9): e1: Nvidia inaugurated its new Beersheva R&D center on 2026-07-07 and completed its move to the new premises. ‖ e2: Globes reported on 2026-07-07 that Nvidia’s Beersheva R&D center covers about 3,000 square meters, three times the previous center’s size, and has hundreds of workstations. ‖ e3: Unnamed sources cited by Globes said the Beersheva center’s building represents an investment of tens of millions of shekels. ‖ e4: Unnamed sources cited by Globes said rent for the Beersheva center is about NIS 2.5 million per year under a 10-year lease. ‖ e5: Nvidia’s Beersheva center has more than 150 employees, and the company plans to hire hundreds more there in the coming years. ‖ e6: Globes reported that Nvidia had over 6,000 employees across five Israeli R&D centers as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e7: Globes reported that Nvidia’s employee count in Israel had tripled since its 2020 acquisition of Mellanox. ‖ e8: Globes reported that Nvidia had more than 450 open positions across its Israeli sites as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e9: Beersheva Mayor Ruvik Danilovich said Nvidia’s decision would expand its activity in the city threefold and create hundreds of jobs.
